Again, 20 per cent of irrigable land in Turkey is located in the GAP region. As such, the GAP region has enormous potential. The GAP today is an integrated regional development project that includes 22 dams, 19 hydropower plants and 1.8 million hectares of waterable spaces, and many sectors based on human and, of course, resources. 13 percent of the hydropower plants under the GAP today have been completed and 74 per cent have been fulfilled. In the large dams that are finished, there is enough water to water 1 million hectares. The construction of the irrigation conduits that will carry this water into the area has been prioritized, and a large portion of it has been completed. It is admirable that 474,528 hectares in the GAP region were opened to water, and a significant part of that happened during our administration. At the watering point in the region, our province of Şanlıurfa has tremendous potential. There are 1.7 million acres in Ceylanpınar TIGEM alone. The opening of the world's number-long irrigation tunnel in Suruç district occurred during the period of our government. Along with the watering areas now, the opening of other plains in the region will reveal tremendous agricultural potential, and the region will again be an important agricultural export centre. The GAP is a national project of Turkey, not just the region, with its enormous potential as well as the region's drinking, economic and political insurance. tbmm-2016-03-01sit01spe0018par0156	Mr. President, dear lawmakers, I salute you all. Today, I'd like to talk to you briefly about the establishment of the DAP Development Administration, its structure, the DAP Action Plan and investments. The DAP, as it is known, is a management of investment and development in the Eastern Anatolian region. The administration was published on 8 June 2011 and in 27958, and was carried out under a decree under the law. On August 17, 2011, the foundations for the functioning of the administration are also redesigned by Code 649 published in the Official Gazette No. 28028. The area of the DAP Administration, a central agency, includes Ağrı, Ardahan, Bingol, Bitlis, Elaziğ, Erzincan, Erzurum, Hakkari, İğdır, Kars, Malatya, Mush, Tunceli and Van. It is based in Erzurum. The aim of the organisation is to ensure that investment projects and activities are conducted in harmony and integrity in order to speed up development of cities in the DAP region, co-ordinate, monitor, and assess the implementation of these projects. The head of the DAP Regional Development Administration is currently one president, two vice presidents and 56 staff members. Again, the duties of the DAP Administration under Code 642 are as follows: 1) To prepare action plans to speed up the development of the region, to coordinate, monitor, and assess the implementation of them. 2) To prepare investment proposals in cooperation with relevant institutions and organisations, make them a priority and send them to the Ministry of Development with relevant institutions and organisations. 3) Watch and evaluate investment projects in the region. 4) Research, study, project and study of the region's potential for development, problems, and possibilities. The basic principles of the DAP Administration: Innovation, transparency, accountability, participation, human focus, openness to cooperation, sustainability, credibility, openness to change, service focus and sensitivity to cultural values. DAP Action Plan: The 2014-2018 DAP Action Plan for the DAP Administration co-ordinating was approved by the Regional Development Supreme Council on December 30, 2014 and was announced to the public on April 25, 2015. This action plan adopts the vision of the Eastern Anatolian Region becoming a high-velocity agricultural and livestock centre. In this framework, the five main development axiss are defined: 1) Increase efficiency and added value in food and agriculture 2) Strengthening industrial and service sector 3) Infrastructure, urbanization and environmental protection 4) Strengthening human and social capital and social infrastructure 5) Enhancing institutional capacity. As part of the DAP Action Plan, a total of 21 billion resources are estimated to be used for 126 actions planned with 52 responsible organisations. As of February 2016, some of the main activities that began in 2013 were supported by the DAP Small-Small Agricultural Watering Programme, which was earmarked for 161.5m euros for small-scale agricultural irrigation projects of public administration from 2013-2015. The aim of the programme is to make irrigation infrastructures for the low-income regional producer, to get them back into agricultural production and to increase productivity in agriculture. The project will continue until 2018. A total of 58.3m euros has been earmarked for the development of animal drinking ponds, plaster production and licensed animal markets, stock markets, sheepkeeping, and support of universities' research and practice units in the DAP region. A total of nine projects will be completed and will be able to build 744,000 square metres of recurrence space through the DAP Area's Proliferation Programme, which was implemented in 2015. These projects began as a pilot application at the request of our Minister of Development Cevdet Yilmaz. Precious lawmakers have been earmarked for research, development and implementation projects in rural areas to promote biogas energy applications and solar power facilities in an effort to reveal the renewable energy potential of the DAP region. As part of the 2015 investment programme to address the social and economic problems of the border-based counties, 13 trillion grants have been earmarked for 27 projects with the Border District Economic and Social Development Programme. According to the years of the DAP Action Plan, there were 398 thousand liras in 2012, 1 million 930,000 in 2013, 488 million in 2014, and 160 million in 2015, 417,000 in 2015; this year's programme is 108 million 533,000. tbmm-2016-03-01sit01spe0020par0190	Mr. President, I have made a commitment to the 2016 budget of our KOP Regional Development Administration. I salute the General Assembly. The KOP Administration was established in 2011 to develop the KOP region, which covers the provinces of Aksaray, Karaman, Iconia and Nicde, but is increasingly threatened by drought. KOP was previously perceived as a pure irrigation project, but was transformed into an integrated regional development project with the establishment of the KOP Administration. The annual rainy average of the KOP region is half the average of Turkey's rain. With climate change, the rains have decreased and become unstable. Meanwhile, the KOP region has only 4 per cent of Turkey's water supply, while Turkey has 12 per cent of agricultural land. For this reason, it is critical to both the region and the country, and it appears to be an area where new water supply projects must be implemented urgently, in addition to the project package known as the Blue Tunnel. Mr. President, valued lawmakers, there's an intense migration to city centers around the world. Since the 1970s, Turkey's economic power has been concentrated in the Marmara Region, and this region has become a region of migration as it buys investments, especially as it buys migrations in Istanbul, and has always attracted attention. In 2003, the education of judges and prosecutors was conducted by the Justice Department's General Directorate of Staff, which was established by the Justice Academy, which was created by the participation of all judicial partners in 2003. This was an important criteria for the independence of the judges' educational processes. During the period until 2004, the Minister of Justice had the authority to give instructions to prosecutors, and with the legal arrangement in 2004, the Minister of Justice was removed from giving instructions to prosecutors. Judge and prosecutors' promotions have been shortened. The order of the Supreme Courts to grade judges and prosecutors over the case has been lifted. Investigator reports on judges and prosecutors have been transparent. The Right to Information Law and the right of judges and prosecutors have been given the right to information on inspection reports and status papers, to appeal, and to prosecute. The interview for the judge and prosecutor's nomination was objective. For the first time, the interview board received a membership from the Supreme Court and the Court. The effect of the written exam has been increased, the interview has been reduced. The HSYK, run by the Ministry of Justice of Secretariat, was restructured in accordance with the institutional, broad-based and democratic representation of the Constitutional Amendment in 2010. The authorities in the Ministry's agreement on judges and prosecutors have all been transferred to HSYK. All of these are studies in harmony with universal principles in ensuring judicial independence and neutrality, in accordance with the basic recommendations of the Council of Europe and the European Union on the judiciary. The referendum, held in 2010, provided a broad restructuring of the HSYK, which consists of members only from high courts. This change was an important opportunity for democratic change in our judicial system, but for two different reasons, this change did not fully take place. First, the CHP took the relevant arrangement to the Constitutional Court. The Constitutional Court broke the election system of this arrangement, which prevented a pluralistic structure from forming. The second was an organized organization that infected the one type and the judicial system. However, with the 2014 election, the partnership of judges and prosecutors with their free will reduced the negatives caused by these two issues. Agreement between political parties over the constitutional amendment must be re-examined by the HSYK structure. European countries reflect parliament's will on the HSYK structure; Belgium, France, the Netherlands, Sweden, Italy, Poland, many countries are able to count. It is clear in our country that some of the members of the HSYK will be favourable by Parliament, some by high courts, some by our President, some by judges and prosecutors who will be elected by their own colleagues. This system will not politicize the judiciary as it is claimed, but it will strengthen its independence and neutrality. One of the things that can be discussed is the distinction between the judges' high council and the prosecutors' high council. There are no European countries where judges and prosecutors are evaluated in the same status. Therefore, it is clear that this arrangement will be in line with European standards. After all, these are suggestions that need to be discussed. With the Ministry of Development, on the one hand, we do the same by diversifying what the State Planning Agency used to do, perhaps more, and, on the other hand, we've created a whole new set of programs. The Ministry of Development has two basic functions: one is to advise the government, to prepare long-term plans, strategies, programmes, at national level, regional and sectoral level; on the other hand, to develop some innovative programs that are subject to implementation. We've started some very important new programs in the last ten years and thirteen years. ONE of them is CYDES. The other is our Water, Sewers and Infrastructure Program, which we call SDUAP. We've created what we call SODES, our Social Support Program. We've created a new program called Attraction Centers for our specific provinces. We've established regional development administrations in addition to the Gap. We established the DAP Regional Development Administration for the Eastern Anatolian Region, established the DOKAP Regional Development Administration for the East Black Sea, and established our COP Administration for the Plain of Conium again. In addition, we've established development agencies again over the past 13 years, and they've activated new mechanisms that include institutions and institutions under the umbrella of our Development Ministry, including local actors in the development process. So today, our Ministry of Development is a ministry in a much wider frame, which makes strategies on one side, and on the other, develops and supports implementation-oriented programmes. Another important function is co-ordinating the investment programme; working on the budget, on the whole budget, on macro balances, on the other hand, especially the general co-ordination of the investment programme. We've been working on that, too. In this case, I'd like to open up the concept of getting up a little bit, if you don't mind. Sometimes, development is only understood as economics. I'd like to stress in particular that it's not like that. Development is a broader concept than economics. As a matter of fact, we have a Ministry of Economics, and we have a Ministry of Development; they're separate ministries. The concept of development, of course, includes the economy, which is a very important element of economic development, but development is not just the economy, but there is social dimension as well as opportunity equality, improving income distribution, increasing social prosperity, as important as economic development, is the essential element of human-focused development. Is social size enough? It's not enough. And then there's the environmental dimension we call foot three. And that represents the balance of generations, not just justice for today's generation, but intergenerational justice, the extent of which we can transfer our assets, nature, and capital to future generations. This is really an essential element of development, sustainable development. These three elements I'm counting are internationally accepted events where the United Nations stands on them, but I stress that they need to be added to a fourth dimension every chance they get, which is the size of governance. We have to put democracy, the state of law, participation in all of this. A full development must include economic, social and environmental dimensions, as well as the size of governance. In this framework, I would like to say that there is a very strong connection between democracy and development. There's a strong connection between justice and development, and there's a strong connection between freedom and development. Here, justice is the foundation of property. We have a beautiful saying, justice is also the basis of development, freedom is also the basis of development. So I want to express in particular that we're looking at development with these concepts. In this framework, the United Nations has 2030 development goals, we share them, we contribute to its formation, and we are now trying to integrate them into our country's policies. You know, they had a Thousand Years of Development Goals, which ended as of 2015, as of last year. Now there are 2030 sustainable development goals for the entire world. Our Development Ministry and our Turkey Statistical Agency are also conducting studies on these goals. We integrate these goals into our policies and study how to follow them. There are 17 main goals, 169 targets, over 200 indicators; we continue to work on all of them. However, in the coming period 2023, we are also in the process of launching more transport work. Now, there's four income groups in the world; there's low income countries, there's a bottom middle income group, there's an upper middle income group, there's a high income group. Turkey is now a country in the upper middle income group -- I'm saying it's an income per person. The goal of our plan is to move Turkey to a high-income league. It's possible for Turkey now, we've come to this threshold. So, you know, sometimes we argue about what we call this middle income trap, our main goal is to move Turkey to the upper income group, to the higher income group, before it falls on it, our vision. And here we respond to these developments in the world with two basic strategies. One is to increase our domestic savings rates, to become less dependent on the world. In Turkey, capital is, unfortunately, not exactly what we want, but capital savings are not exactly what we want. Because our internal savings are inadequate, we have to finance our investments with the savings of the outside world. Which, of course, poses an obvious problem, causing us a problem of external debt. So, we've been implementing important policies recently to increase our domestic savings rates and finance our investments with solid resources. And in the essence of the transformation programs that I just mentioned, there's actually a question of increasing these savings rates. We've been seeing the consequences of that lately. In 2014, our domestic savings increased by 15 per cent to national income. Over the past years, he was down to 13 percent. The value in 2015 was up to 15.6%. We expect this to go on in years to come. That's a reduction in current deficits, actually, of domestic savings. Second, of course, to direct our increasing savings to more productive areas. In this framework, we are also trying to improve the investment climate by improving a number of reforms, the Istanbul financial centre, the strengthening of the individual pension system, and accession banking with a number of instruments, while on the other hand, we are trying to improve the investment climate to improve Turkey into a more attractive country. This is the constitutional duty of the Constitutional Court, but I would like to read from here the rules of the Constitutional Court on individual applications: the common law must be exhausted in order to apply for individual application according to the Constitution. The individual application must be exhausted before the individual application of administrative and judicial applications, which have been proposed in the law for action or negligence, should be exhausted. This is the rule of law; the first two are the Constitutional provisions; and cannot be disclosed without the basis of the invalid decisions. The Constitutional Court cannot accept, and cannot submit individual applications on the issue of administrative and judicial application, according to the laws of the Constitution and the laws. It is an uncontroversial rule that he makes an inadmissible decision and rejects individual applications during trial. The Constitutional Court, which has taken over the individual application on the basis of a decision of acceptance, cannot observe the issues that need to be observed in accordance with the law. I mean, he can't put himself in the first-degree court and do what the first-degree court should do in the case, put himself in the court of appeals and do what the Supreme Court must do. If the Constitutional Court does this in an individual application study, then it will bring him to a position like a first-degree court or a higher appeals court. When we look at some of the Supreme Court's previous decisions and recent violations, the law has reviewed the application and decided to violate it before it was exhausted, even before the indictment was read, and before the trial began.
